# Contradictions and collisions

What the inventoried artifacts give differently, found and checked for the Artifact Textbook (issue #120, finished under #136), rendered on 2026-10-23. Readers went over the inventory's numbers table for every quantity two artifacts give with different values, and over each topic cluster's full records for disagreements, revised figures, stale statements, duplicated texts and shared unsourced claims. The table holds 3,025 numbers from 69 artifacts. The number readers' notes record reading two parts of it, 1,231 and 956 rows, 2,187 in all; the parts and the rule that split them are not kept in this repository, so which numbers went unread is not known here.

A script then looked for every reader's quote in the artifact's extracted text and, failing that, in the page's own files. It ignores case and spacing, quotation marks, square and round brackets and the marks of tables and Markdown (the bar, asterisk, underscore, backtick, hash and greater-than sign), and reads typographic characters and a few accented letters as their plain ones; every other mark, braces and the less-than sign among them, must match. It matches around an ellipsis or an editor's words in square brackets, leaving unchecked any part shorter than 12 characters, and a quote longer than 48 characters that it finds neither whole nor in parts counts as partly found when one of its 48-character stretches is in the text; the columns "Reader's quote A" and "B" say what it found. Then a skeptic read both sources of every cluster candidate, and of every number collision a reader classed as a contradiction or a later revision and 11 others #136's first run took, and gave a verdict with the exact words it rests on, copied from each source. Those words are on the cluster pages and under the number tables beside the reader's, with where the script found them. Last, a sample of the verdicts was read again by agents told to overturn them, and then every number collision on the confirmed list that the sample left out, with the rows that read the same figures ruled again together (How far the verdicts hold, below).

Rows count from 0, as the record and the skeptics' explanations do, so "candidate 4" in an explanation is row 4 of its cluster's page. Nothing here is copied from memory. People other than published authors are named by role, and no e-mail address, phone number, or home or street address is reproduced; where a quote named a person or carried such a detail, an editor's words in square brackets stand in its place.

## How the verdicts are read

For a cluster candidate:

- confirmed: both sources say what the candidate says, which depends on the reader's kind. For a contradiction they disagree. For a duplicated text the same text or figure is carried by both, so it is one claim and not two confirmations. For a shared unsourced claim the claim is in each source and no source for it is given on those pages. For a stale statement a later source, or the artifact's own later section, shows it no longer holds.
- superseded: one source is later, by its date or because it plainly follows the other, and revises the earlier one.
- refuted: they agree once read in context, over a different scope, subset, unit, interval or date that the pages state, or a statement or quote is not in its source.
- cannot tell: the texts do not settle it; the hint says what would.

For a number collision:

- confirmed-contradiction: the same quantity over the same scope, with different values.
- updated-later: the later source, by its date or because it plainly follows the other, gives a new figure for the same quantity.
- same-number-restated: the values agree once read, by rounding or another way of writing the figure.
- scope-or-unit-differs: the same name over a different subset, denominator, unit, interval, version or date, as the pages state.
- different-quantities: not the same thing at all.
- cannot-tell: the texts do not settle it; the hint says what would.

A blank verdict means no skeptic read that candidate, and the reader's kind is then all there is.

## How far the verdicts hold

On 2026-10-23 the skeptics' verdicts were themselves read again, the earlier account's verifiers' among them. The sample was drawn by stratum, the 14 clusters and the numbers, and within each by the SHA-1 of the UTF-8 string "stratum\|row", rows counted from 0 and the numbers' stratum named Number collisions, lowest first: from every stratum two verdicts that put a candidate on the confirmed list and one that kept a candidate off it, refuted or a number's agreement, then the 5 such dismissals and the 4 cannot-tell verdicts with the lowest hashes left across all strata, 54 in all; and the 4 verdicts whose own quotes the script could not place, 58 in all. work/tools/sample_review.py draws it again from the record as it stood at commit 1c656d4, since the review's rulings and the second reading of ten later moved verdicts between sides. Five re-checkers, three for the verdicts that put a candidate on the list and two for the rest, each told to overturn every verdict in its share, read both sources of each and gave their own verdict, whether the hint was right and the words they rest on. Where a re-checker overturned a verdict or its hint, one of three judges read both readings and the sources and ruled; all 11 rulings sided with the re-checker.

- Verdicts that put a candidate on the confirmed list, drawn at random: 26 of 30 held (87 %, Wilson 95 % interval 70 to 95 %).
- Verdicts that kept a candidate off it, drawn at random: 22 of 24 held (92 %, Wilson 95 % interval 74 to 98 %).
- Verdicts drawn because the script could not place one of their own quotes: 3 of 4 overturned.

In all 9 verdicts were overturned and 2 held with a corrected hint. Each now reads as the ruling has it, with the verdict it replaced in the Review column of its row. The rest were not read again, but for the confirmed number collisions, which the follow-up below read whole: by the rates of the draws from the strata that still hold them (26 of 28 and 22 of 24 held), between 2 and 23 % of the other confirmed verdicts may be wrong, and between 2 and 26 % of the others, so read a row before relying on it.

These rates are unweighted: every stratum gave the same number of verdicts whatever its size. Weighted by how many of the 847 verdicts neither the review nor the follow-up read each stratum holds, about 92 % of the 416 on the confirmed list and 91 % of the 431 others are expected to hold, with wider intervals than the counts above, since a few drawn verdicts stand for each stratum. Most of the unreviewed confirmed verdicts that may be wrong are in Detectors, architecture and the training plan: 1 of its 2 drawn was overturned, and it holds 39 of the 416, so read those first.

- [Datasets, holdings and licences](contradictions/datasets-holdings-and-licences.md), row 63: refuted held, with a corrected hint.
- [Detectors, architecture and the training plan](contradictions/detectors-architecture-and-the-training-plan.md), row 30: superseded-by-later became refuted.
- [Level naming and counting](contradictions/level-naming-and-counting.md), row 80: confirmed became refuted.
- number collision 30: scope-or-unit-differs became confirmed-contradiction.
- number collision 110: confirmed-contradiction became scope-or-unit-differs.
- number collision 173: updated-later became scope-or-unit-differs.
- [The case for the OR study: proposal, seed grant and IRB protocols](contradictions/the-case-for-the-or-study-proposal-seed-grant-and-irb-protoc.md), row 37: refuted became confirmed.
- [Transitional vertebrae and border variants](contradictions/transitional-vertebrae-and-border-variants.md), row 6: confirmed became superseded-by-later.
- [Verification benches, overnight audits and OR readiness](contradictions/verification-benches-overnight-audits-and-or-readiness.md), row 37: confirmed became refuted.
- [Verification benches, overnight audits and OR readiness](contradictions/verification-benches-overnight-audits-and-or-readiness.md), row 88: cannot-tell became confirmed.

10 number verdicts of #136's first run were cannot-tell because their values were missing from the plain text, which cuts each line of a page's script data at 600 characters. A skeptic read them again in the artifacts' own files: 6 scope-or-unit-differs, 2 confirmed-contradiction, 2 same-number-restated (collisions 2, 3, 4, 7, 8, 26, 77, 78, 126, 182). Each says read again in its Review column, with the verdict it replaced.

### Every confirmed number collision, read again

On 2026-10-23 every number collision on the confirmed list that the review's sample left out, 55 in all (24 confirmed-contradiction, 31 updated-later), was read again (issue #155) as the review read its sample, with three differences. Five re-checkers read them, each given every fifth row, where the review gave its confirmed verdicts to three; each artifact whose page changed after the inventory read it was marked so, with what changed; and the re-checkers' brief, otherwise the review's, added one sentence: "This reading is the follow-up of issue #155: an earlier review drew two of these confirmed number verdicts at random and overturned both, so every one it left out is now read again." That sentence told the re-checkers that such verdicts had failed before, which may have made them readier to overturn, so the follow-up's rates are its own and not to be pooled with the review's. Each re-checker, told to overturn every verdict in its share and its hint, read both sources of each row and gave its own verdict, whether the hint was right and the words it rests on. Where a re-checker overturned a verdict or its hint, one of three judges read both readings and the sources and ruled; of the 20 rulings, 17 with the re-checker, 2 with the skeptic, 1 with neither. work/tools/reread_numbers.py writes the re-checkers' input again from the record as #137 merged it (commit fde5b06), and out/reread-confirmed-numbers.json holds each reading and ruling. The Any-View Spine Plan page changed three times after the inventory read it, all on 2026-10-22 and none in an accuracy figure: at 20:03 UTC its census paragraph, the per-region table of its section 4 and the next step of its section 5, and at 23:31 and 23:43 UTC its table of paired X-ray and CT sets and two notes under it. The re-checkers of rows 105, 106 and 107, which quote it, read it as it is now, with a note that named only the last two changes. The page is rendered from the artifacts' text as it is now, so one quote of the Renders cluster, row 45, is no longer found, the page writing 'unused; registration' where it wrote 'unused. Registration'.

On 2026-10-23 a review of the follow-up found that rows reading the same figures or the same pages, ruled one at a time, did not agree with one another, and that two rulings rested on scopes the pages do not state. They were ruled again in five groups: naming on whole lateral C-ArmLSpine frames (rows 0, 5, 6, 104, 105, 106 and 107); the 8,422 held-out crops (rows 61 and 111); the T12 and L1 shape test (rows 52 and 171); the coordbench plan's pairs and arms (rows 226 and 227); scopes the pages state or the readers computed (rows 24 and 31). One judge for each group read all of its rows together, each with both readings, its ruling and its verdict as the follow-up left it, and every source, the artifacts the review cited among them, with the review's points given as claims to check against the texts and never to take as true; the judge kept each row's verdict and hint or ruled it again. Of the 15 rows, 3 kept their verdict and hint and 12 were ruled again; rows 20 and 110, shown to the judges beside them, stand as they were. work/tools/reread_numbers.py writes each judge's input again from the reading and the follow-up's input. What each group's judge found:

- Naming on whole lateral C-ArmLSpine frames, rows 0, 5, 6, 104, 105, 106 and 107: The 22 September pages (OR Readiness Ledger, Counting From The Sacrum) carry one set of C-ArmLSpine naming figures: 98.2% whole lateral frame, 72.8% with one vertebra, 96.3% zero-shot lateral, 37.9% zero-shot AP. The October pages (Research Directions and the handoff of 8 October, the Any-View plan of 21 October) carry another: 97.4%, 74.8%, 96.4% (595/617) and 43.7%, and the handoff says its results catalog wins over any other file. So the book should carry the October set as the later record and the September set as earlier, on the same verdict across rows 5, 6, 105, 106 and 107. The one dissent is State of the Literature (8 October), which still calls 98.2% (606 of 617 lateral vertebrae) the best number; that '606 of 617' is its wording, not the ledger's. Research Directions and the handoff give 98.2% to the coupled count (97.0% plus 1.2 points), but no page says whether the September whole-frame 98.2% was the same measure under another name. The September record's case count stays open: 148 in the ledger against 141 with 617 vertebrae on the sacrum page, to be settled from the CV fold record.
- The 8,422 held-out crops, rows 61 and 111: All three pages score the same 8,422 held-out real-film crops, split CSXA 7,440 and NHANES 982. The Level Context Ladder (13 September) gives 96.7% exact and 99.7% within one at one vertebra visible for arms that join films to synthetic crops. The Reality Check (15 September) and the Corpus Audit (14 September) give 98.7% exact and 99.8% within one for a films-only model scored one crop at a time, against 99.4% for its recurrent head; the audit says that model's training bank holds films alone. The book should carry each figure with its model and scope, which makes the three rows scope-or-unit-differs on a stated model difference, and it should not say the 98.7% matches a wider rung of the ladder, which no page states and no row matches exactly. Two questions stay open: the context rung of the films-only model's crops, which the run file or model id would settle, and why the Reality Check says 'no thoracic' when the ladder counts 66 thoracic crops in the same 8,422. Beside them, row 110 agrees: Its verdict, scope-or-unit-differs, and its hint (98.7% is the films-only model scored one crop at a time against 99.4% for its run head, and no page names that model's context rung) agree with rows 61 and 111. Its explanation says 'single crop' does not mean one vertebra in view, but no page says that. The ruling rests better on the stated model difference: the ladder's arms are films joined to synthetic crops, and the films-only model's bank holds films alone.
- The T12 and L1 shape test, rows 52 and 171: Both rows set the same two passages side by side: 1485 vertebrae behind the reported T12-versus-L1 AUC 0.990 and 97.3% accuracy in Counting Against Two Anchors (undated), and 1,486 T12 and L1 vertebrae from 798 cases as the n and power of the planned Stages 1 and 2 in Research Directions (2026-10-08), which repeats 1,486 in its PAR-1 and PAR-2 rows. Research Directions separates its planned ratio-only and projected runs from the published 0.990 by features and view, but never gives the published test's n and never says its planned runs count a different set of vertebrae, so the one-vertebra gap is not explained by either page; both rows are therefore cannot-tell, and row 52's scope-or-unit-differs is changed to match row 171. The book should carry 1485 with the 0.990 result from Counting Against Two Anchors and 1,486 from 798 cases only as Research Directions' planned sample, never one in place of the other. The open question is whether the 0.990 run's input and the set the planned stages read (on v11 for Stage 2) are the same vertebrae; a row count of each settles it.
- The coordbench plan's pairs and arms, rows 226 and 227: The Symphony Handoff (2026-10-13) plans coordbench's first benchmark in three dispatches: a survey of which of the 50 pre-registered flash pairs grade on runners, a pilot, then five arms (solo, coop, coop-git, team, sdlc) on every pair that grades, '42 known, up to 50', sized at 378 sessions and powered on 42 pairs. Its 294 sessions and about $125 for four arms are quoted only as the kit's own estimate. Symphony in Brief (2026-10-14) reports the survey done (tag survey-1) and the pre-registered run as 50 pairs from 20 tasks under the same five arms. So 42 is the handoff's own provisional planning count, which the later in-brief settles at 50, and the arms agree across both pages. The book should carry five arms and 50 pairs from the in-brief, with 42 and 378 sessions as the handoff's earlier sizing; the texts leave open the session count and cost for the 50-pair run, which coordbench's PLAN.md at the cited commit or a run record would have to state.
- Scopes the pages state or the readers computed, rows 24 and 31: In all three rows the two pages give one fixed quantity of one dataset different values, and the scope that would reconcile them is something a reader worked out, not something either page states. For BUU-LSPINE the atlas gives 129 four-lumbar and 15 six-lumbar among 3,600 patients; the decisions page gives 130 sacral and 15 lumbar with no total, and only a computed sum ties its counts to the atlas's 3,606 lateral csv files. For the NHANES II annotations zip the handoff's card says 1.2 GB and the atlas says 1,017,496,285 bytes; the handoff's footer gives 1.2 GB for a two-file folder, but the card does not say its figure is that total. So rows 24 and 31 are confirmed contradictions, like row 20. The book should carry the atlas's figures, credited to the atlas, with the other values flagged and the inferred explanations noted as what a record has to confirm. Still open: a recount of the L4L6 sheet by patient, a per-file listing of the nhanes_annotated folder, and a count of the Mendeley Dataset.zip by file type. Beside them, row 20 agrees: Confirmed-contradiction fits: the atlas counts 714 jpg and lists Mendeley-716 as an alias while the handoff gives 716 images (208 + 508), and neither page states a scope that sets them apart. Its hint, a file count of the archive before choosing, matches how rows 24 and 31 are now read.

- Verdicts that were confirmed-contradiction: 13 of 24 held (54 %, Wilson 95 % interval 35 to 72 %), 4 of them with a corrected hint.
- Verdicts that were updated-later: 28 of 31 held (90 %, Wilson 95 % interval 75 to 97 %), 2 of them with a corrected hint.
- All 55: 41 of 55 held (75 %, Wilson 95 % interval 62 to 84 %).

The counts are exact for these rows, since every one was read; the intervals take the 55 as a sample of the verdicts such skeptics give, so they say how well that rate is known, not how many of these rows may be wrong. Of the 55, 46 stay on the confirmed list and 9 left it.

In all 14 verdicts were overturned and 6 held with a corrected hint. Each now reads as its ruling has it, or its group's judge where the group re-rule ruled it again, and its Review column gives the follow-up's outcome with the verdict it replaced. With these, every number collision on the confirmed list has been read by a skeptic and then again, by the review or the follow-up.

## Confirmed: what a planner must not take from one artifact alone

Every candidate a skeptic confirmed or found revised, by cluster. Disagreements, revisions and stale statements come first; duplicated texts and shared unsourced claims follow, confirmed in the senses above, which are not disagreements: a duplicated text is one claim and not two confirmations, and a shared unsourced claim has no source on either page. Each item gives its row on the cluster's page, its kind, its verdict and the outcome of the review or the follow-up where either read it, the two artifacts, and the value or statement the skeptic says the book should carry. The whole candidate, with both readings and both sets of quotes, is on the cluster's page.

### [Level naming and counting](contradictions/level-naming-and-counting.md)

Disagreements, revisions and stale statements:

- Row 0, contradiction, confirmed (registration-free-level-id-feasibility-uramo1 and naming-vertebrae-without-ct-hjy4vc): Huang 2022 registration-free cadaver long-film accuracy (88.3%) as the published comparator. Carry 88.3% only as the figure Huang 2022 reports on 100 cadaveric Long-Film images, marked unverified as the feasibility page says, until the paper's full text is read.
- Row 4, contradiction, confirmed (counting-from-the-sacrum and or-readiness-ledger): C-ArmLSpine case count. The fold file of the five-fold cross-validation would settle it. Until then carry 148 cases from the ledger (split 105/15/28), give 149 as the dataset's case count, and flag 141.
- Row 5, contradiction, superseded-by-later (counting-from-the-sacrum and handoff-schwingnet-ctspinopelvic1k-and-the-imaging-programme-rbcdmf): Whole-lateral-frame exact-level accuracy on C-ArmLSpine (count alone, coupling, bodies vs frames). Carry 97.4% from the handoff (2026-10-08) for fine-tuned naming on whole lateral frames. Give 98.2% only as the coupled count on anchored bodies, with its 3-case gain over the count alone.
- Row 6, superseded-number, superseded-by-later (counting-from-the-sacrum and any-view-spine-plan): Zero-shot AP naming accuracy on C-ArmLSpine. Carry 43.7% from the any-view plan (2026-10-21).
- Row 7, superseded-number, superseded-by-later (counting-from-the-sacrum and handoff-schwingnet-ctspinopelvic1k-and-the-imaging-programme-rbcdmf): One-vertebra-in-field naming accuracy. Carry 74.8% from the handoff (2026-10-08).
- Row 8, superseded-number, superseded-by-later (counting-from-the-sacrum and any-view-spine-plan): Zero-shot lateral naming on C-ArmLSpine. Carry 96.4% (595/617) from the any-view plan.
- Row 9, contradiction, superseded-by-later (or-readiness-ledger and any-view-spine-plan): Held-out-device vertebra detection recall on C-ArmLSpine. Carry 80.5% from the handoff (2026-10-08) as held-out-device recall, noting that the ledger's 90.6% was an outline-convention re-score on 56 frames. The catalog row's truth convention would explain the gap.
- Row 11, contradiction, confirmed (counting-levels-under-fluoroscopy and aidas-seed-grant-proposal): Population-matched render-trained sacrum detection on DeepFluoro real frames. Carry 90-97% from counting-levels, with its table, unless the run file shows 96-98%. The proposal's range needs a source or a correction.
- Row 12, contradiction, confirmed (counting-levels-under-fluoroscopy and aidas-seed-grant-proposal): Fraction of a real frame's variance the physics renderer explains. Carry counting-levels' corrected values: 86% for physics alone and about 90% for the calibrated renderer. Neither page says whether the 93.9% smooth-field figure also moves.
- Row 13, contradiction, confirmed (counting-levels-under-fluoroscopy and naming-vertebrae-without-ct-hjy4vc): What decides the level: counting from an anchor versus naming from morphology. Present them as successive designs: naming from morphology (2026-09-18), then counting from the anchor (counting-levels, AIDaS). Give the handoff's combined model as the current state.
- Row 15, contradiction, superseded-by-later (naming-vertebrae-without-an-anchor-3dfzmh and vertebral-levels-without-ct-a4rydj): Sweep chain-intact rate by overlap (half, one, two, four bodies). Carry the re-measured table of 2026-09-18 for accuracy and exposures. Flag the chain-intact column, where the naming page's values differ.
- Row 16, contradiction, superseded-by-later (naming-vertebrae-without-an-anchor-3dfzmh and vertebral-levels-without-ct-a4rydj): Thoracic two-vertebra naming with and without the sweep. Carry the re-measured n=12 result and the naming page's wider table. The two September-18 pages disagree on the same 12 studies (4.3->91.3 vs 8.3->91.7), so the evaluation file should decide.
- Row 17, contradiction, superseded-by-later (naming-vertebrae-without-an-anchor-3dfzmh and naming-vertebrae-without-ct-hjy4vc): Lumbar two-vertebra naming on CT renders with the sweep. Carry 76.9% to 93.9% on 118 held-out lumbar CT renders (2026-09-18).
- Row 18, contradiction, confirmed (naming-vertebrae-without-ct-hjy4vc and vertebral-levels-without-ct-a4rydj): Automatic detector's per-body recall on held-out radiographs. Neither value can be carried as it stands. The detector's evaluation file (misses over the denominator) settles it; three of 243 would be 98.8%.
- Row 19, contradiction, superseded-by-later (vertebral-levels-without-ct-a4rydj and naming-vertebrae-without-ct-hjy4vc): Whether the sweep with the automatic detector beats a single frame. Carry the naming page's 90.4% (automatic detector, three vertebrae, held-out real radiographs). Give 66.0/72.3 only as the state before the linking fix.
- Row 20, contradiction, superseded-by-later (vertebral-levels-without-ct-a4rydj and naming-vertebrae-without-ct-hjy4vc): Size of the linking-rule fix (duplicate suppression) on chain naming. Carry 67.4% to 91.8% from the naming page.

### [FluoroCap: the corpus, its pipeline and its audits](contradictions/fluorocap-the-corpus-its-pipeline-and-its-audits.md)

Disagreements, revisions and stale statements:

- Row 0, contradiction, confirmed (fluorocap-dataset-card-xwd4wx and fluorocap-corpus-audit-9hjzzs): Figure-mate region agreement with constraint decoding versus the level model alone (same corpus, same date, 2026-09-14). Carry 91.5% (519 pairs) for the decoder and 52.1% (468 pairs) for the model alone, as agreement between automatic sources rather than accuracy. Mark the card's 88% as a typed number with no source. Regenerating the card from the decode's consistency file would settle the figure.
- Row 1, contradiction, superseded-by-later (fluorocap-corpus-audit-9hjzzs and fluorocap-reality-check-czudvb): What the panels without a vertebra box are: detector misses or non-radiographs. Carry the reality check's measured purity, with its file and time, and drop the audit's inference. How much of the no-box set is detector miss stays unmeasured until a human reads a stratified sample of those panels.
- Row 2, contradiction, superseded-by-later (fluorocap-corpus-audit-9hjzzs and fluorocap-reality-check-czudvb): The 2,143-panel held-out set behind the 93% modality score: FluoroCap panels or film-manifest classes. Carry: 93.1% on 2,143 held-out film-manifest items, not FluoroCap panels. The classifier is unvalidated on the corpus until a labelled FluoroCap sample is scored, and the book should name which classifier version each number belongs to.
- Row 3, superseded-number, superseded-by-later (fluorocap-corpus-audit-9hjzzs and fluorocap-reality-check-czudvb): Caption-held-out accuracy of the level model alone, and containment of its level set. Carry the reality check's numbers together with its caveat, and present neither pair as an accuracy. The random draw on the level verification bench, once it has been read, gives the first accuracy estimate.
- Row 5, contradiction, confirmed (fluorocap-dataset-card-xwd4wx and fluorocap-access-setup-jlvkft): Provenance of the corpus versus the closed-journal refetch and the Instagram harvest. Withdraw the card's open-licence sentence. Carry the later records: the licence is 'the publishers' (figures)' (registry) and 'figure reuse only, never redistributed' (atlas), and the corpus holds closed-journal copies fetched through the WSU proxy plus Instagram and Reddit images.
- Row 6, contradiction, confirmed (fluorocap-access-setup-jlvkft and schwingnet-data-registry): Rule for sources whose terms forbid automated collection. Carry the later rule: no scripted collection where a platform's terms forbid it, and copies already taken are held for private screening only until permission arrives. This matches the project decision that Radiopaedia is never fetched by script. Record a status for the 1,633 Instagram images, which no page gives one.
- Row 7, contradiction, superseded-by-later (fluorocap-dataset-card-xwd4wx and the-lab-recipe-1t2gvp): Releasing FluoroCap (Dataset A and B licence tiers) versus the private-forever rule. Carry the private-forever rule. Describe Datasets A and B as internal frozen versions. Any public release would need a new decision from the owner.
- Row 8, contradiction, superseded-by-later (fluorocap-corpus-audit-9hjzzs and fluorocap-reality-check-czudvb): Whether FluoroCap may train the level model. Carry directive D1: FluoroCap may be a test set at most and is never training data. Add the handoff's finding that the old C-ArmLSpine fold banks still held FluoroCap crops.
- Row 10, contradiction, superseded-by-later (overnight-the-honest-99-kantj8 and handoff-schwingnet-ctspinopelvic1k-and-the-imaging-programme-rbcdmf): The 99% selective-accuracy claim and its held-out status. Carry the 99% as record only, because it comes from a leaked bank. Any selective-accuracy claim must be rerun on the G3 person-disjoint split and pass through the evaluation gate before it is quoted.
- Row 12, contradiction, superseded-by-later (overnight-the-honest-99-kantj8 and fluorocap-reality-check-czudvb): Whether any real panel carries an L6 or T13 label. Carry the 16 Sep counts as decoder placements that no human has checked, and state that no real panel carries a human-verified L6 or T13. Reading the 338 L6 and 254 T13 panels, or the transitional bench, would settle whether any real positive exists.
- Row 14, superseded-number, superseded-by-later (fluorocap-overnight-report-xnhgdo and fluorocap-reality-check-czudvb): Images on disk after the refetch (figure panels, video, video-medium). Carry 19,581 (18,119 figure panels, 1,096 video, 366 video-medium) as of 15 Sep at about 07:00, with how it was counted. Later counts (Honest 99, atlas) supersede it in turn.
- Row 15, superseded-number, superseded-by-later (fluorocap-reality-check-czudvb and fluoroscopy-data-atlas): Corpus size over time: 11,349 to 19,581 to 20,700 decoded to 29,500 panels. Carry the atlas and registry counts as current, and date every earlier count. Name the command behind 29,500 (presumably a find on the grid tree), which neither page states.
- Row 16, superseded-number, superseded-by-later (fluorocap-overnight-report-xnhgdo and fluorocap-reality-check-czudvb; review: held): Closed-journal refetch: figures replaced and pixel gain. - Row 17, superseded-number, superseded-by-later (fluorocap-overnight-report-xnhgdo and fluorocap-reality-check-czudvb): Video panels in the corpus. Carry 1,096 video and 366 video-medium for the morning of 15 Sep, and 2,535 video frames for 16 Sep, each with its date.
- Row 18, superseded-number, superseded-by-later (fluorocap-dataset-card-xwd4wx and fluorocap-reality-check-czudvb): Manifest rows and dataset version. - Row 0, contradiction, confirmed (counting-levels-under-fluoroscopy and aidas-seed-grant-proposal): DeepFluoro sacrum recall for a detector trained on renders of DeepFluoro's own CTs (leave-one-specimen-out). Carry 90-97% from Counting Levels Under Fluoroscopy, the figure tied to the dated runs and a named report tool. Use 96-98% only if a run record for it turns up.
- Row 2, contradiction, confirmed (counting-levels-under-fluoroscopy and aidas-seed-grant-proposal): Fraction of a real frame's variance explained by the physics renderer. Carry the corrected reading from Counting Levels Under Fluoroscopy (86% physics alone, about 90% calibrated on held-out frames). Mark the grant's and protocol's 80.9% as the figure from before the correction.
- Row 5, contradiction, confirmed (counting-levels-under-fluoroscopy and aidas-seed-grant-proposal): Results of the learned refiners and the Fourier low-band swap. Carry the measured results from Counting Levels Under Fluoroscopy, with its caveat that every gain is inside the seed spread. Drop the grant's 'results are pending'.
- Row 9, contradiction, confirmed (protocol-1-eprotocol-draft-mkmh7p and level-counting-visual-abstract): Where the registry's wrong-level events occurred. Carry the regional rates from the grant and the visual abstract. Drop 'every event was at L4-5 or L5-S1' unless the registry paper supports it for some subset.
- Row 14, contradiction, confirmed (level-counting-visual-abstract and aidas-seed-grant-proposal): What $1,750-1,950 per fiducial placement means. Carry the grant's derivation: about $1,750-1,950 per placement, which is 51.9 minutes of OR time at the FY2014 cost, not a price for the device.
- Row 17, contradiction, superseded-by-later (protocol-1-eprotocol-draft-mkmh7p and retrospective-fluoroscopy-protocol): IRB review route for the retrospective fluoroscopy collection. Carry Expedited Category 5 from the Retrospective Fluoroscopy Protocol. Note that its FDA-ineligibility reason cites no regulation (see candidate 48).
- Row 19, contradiction, superseded-by-later (protocol-1-eprotocol-draft-mkmh7p and retrospective-fluoroscopy-protocol): Records requested and the date window of the retrospective pull. Carry the retrospective protocol's scope (one surgeon's spine cases, 2021-2026, N to confirm by pilot query). Treat the 2006 window and the 40,000-study figure as the superseded design.
- Row 20, contradiction, superseded-by-later (protocol-1-eprotocol-draft-mkmh7p and retrospective-fluoroscopy-protocol): Study duration. Carry the retrospective protocol's plan (steps 2-11 within six months, proposed end two years after approval, to confirm). Leave out the draft's three years.
- Row 21, contradiction, superseded-by-later (protocol-1-eprotocol-draft-mkmh7p and retrospective-fluoroscopy-protocol): Reference standard for level truth. Carry the retrospective protocol's reference standard (operative note, post-operative imaging where needed) and its H1 of at least 97% recall. Mark the CT-registration standard as the earlier design.
- Row 22, contradiction, superseded-by-later (protocol-1-eprotocol-draft-mkmh7p and retrospective-fluoroscopy-protocol): Sample size for the primary endpoint. Carry about 1,100 labelled held-out bodies (97%, half-width 1 point) from the Retrospective Fluoroscopy Protocol. Note that its case count waits on the 20-case pilot query.
- Row 24, contradiction, confirmed (protocol-1-eprotocol-draft-mkmh7p and fluorocap-reality-check-czudvb; review: held): Frames and patients the pull should yield. Carry neither as settled. The pilot PACS query of 20 cases named in the Retrospective Fluoroscopy Protocol (frames per case, share missing from PACS) decides the yield.
- Row 25, contradiction, superseded-by-later (protocol-1-eprotocol-draft-mkmh7p and retrospective-fluoroscopy-protocol): Whether Protocol 2 (shadow-mode) is submitted with Protocol 1. Carry the retrospective protocol's sequence: Protocol 2 is submitted separately, after Protocol 1 meets its endpoints.
- Row 32, contradiction, confirmed (counting-from-the-sacrum and or-readiness-ledger): Number of C-ArmLSpine cases in the five-fold evaluation. Settle it with the fold lists of the cross-validation (cases with a lateral frame scored). Until then, carry 98.2% with the case count marked unresolved (141 or 148 cases; 120 distinct subjects).
- Row 36, contradiction, superseded-by-later (aidas-seed-grant-proposal and vertebral-levels-without-ct-a4rydj): What SchwingNet does: count without naming, or name without counting. Carry 'count, not name' from the grant and the protocol as the method under validation. Treat Vertebral Levels Without CT as an earlier, naming-based design.
- Row 37, contradiction, confirmed (protocol-1-eprotocol-draft-mkmh7p and fluoroscopy-data-atlas; review: overturned, was refuted): Existence of public intraoperative spine fluoroscopy with level labels. Do not carry the protocol draft's statement unqualified. Add from the Fluoroscopy Data Atlas that it records an unverified open download of nine level-named intraoperative C-arm patients (NIT Warangal / NIMS Hyderabad; 8-bit overlays beside DRRs; level names from preoperative CT centroids). Add that it also records VerSeg-Net's 50 lumbar fluoroscopy patients with per-level masks, sharing route not recorded. Note that C-ArmLSpine's level names are derived by the project, not shipped. Reading the Warangal repository would settle whether it meets the draft's definition.
- Row 39, contradiction, confirmed (fluorocap-reality-check-czudvb and registration-free-level-id-feasibility-uramo1): Huang 2022 registration-free naming at 88.3%. Do not carry 88.3% as a verified comparator. Describe the 2022 study by its protocol alone, as the Registration-Free Level ID Feasibility note does.
- Row 59, stale, confirmed (protocol-1-eprotocol-draft-mkmh7p and protocol-1-eprotocol-draft-mkmh7p): Older eProtocol draft of 15 September 2026. Carry the Retrospective Fluoroscopy Protocol's fields. Mark the September draft as superseded, so its Exempt 4, 2006 window, fracture aim and registry linkage are not copied back in.
- Row 62, stale, confirmed (fluoroscopy-data-atlas and schwingnet-grid-pipeline): Request letters and replies for outside data. Carry the request status from the 21 October grid pipeline (Radiopaedia signed and paid, waiting on countersignature), and take per-letter sent status from the spine-datasets records rather than the atlas.

- Row 54, duplicate-text, confirmed (counting-levels-under-fluoroscopy and level-counting-visual-abstract): Opening line: 1 in 830, 1 in 240 thoracic, half of surveyed surgeons, 81% a count gone wrong. - Row 1, superseded-number, confirmed (celebrate-life-flyer-noutom and celebrate-life-scoliosis-night-julcos): Celebrate Life start time and hours on Saturday, October 24. Carry 5:00-7:00 PM from the scoliosis flyer if the organiser confirms that flyer is current.
- Row 2, contradiction, confirmed (celebrate-life-flyer-noutom and celebrate-life-scoliosis-night-julcos): The Halloween event Celebrate Life runs alongside: its name, host and setting. Confirm the companion event's name, host and setting with the venue. Carry the scoliosis flyer's indoor Turf or Treat if it is the current version.
- Row 3, contradiction, confirmed (celebrate-life-flyer-noutom and celebrate-life-scoliosis-night-julcos): Whether the organisers feed families who sign up. Carry the scoliosis flyer's arrangement (food bought at the venue) if it is current, and confirm with the venue whether the organisers provide any food.
- Row 4, contradiction, confirmed (celebrate-life-flyer-noutom and celebrate-life-scoliosis-night-julcos): Venue-side social handle printed on the flyer. Carry the account that matches the confirmed host of the companion event (the Fieldhouse on the current flyer).
- Row 19, contradiction, confirmed (osc-outreach-funding-kak2gk and osc-outreach-funding-kak2gk): Which student fund to ask first: Senate or Alumni (internal to the funding page). Carry the order the page's own steps give: ask for alumni funds through Student Affairs first, then send the Senate request naming what was tried.
- Row 20, contradiction, confirmed (osc-outreach-funding-kak2gk and osc-outreach-funding-kak2gk): 'The first seven are local and small' versus the amounts in entries six and seven (internal). Carry the page's groups by speed (days, weeks, months) and drop 'local and small' for the AOA and Community Foundation entries.
- Row 22, contradiction, confirmed (celebrate-life-scoliosis-night-julcos and aidas-seed-grant-proposal): The owner's department as printed for outreach versus the grant. Confirm the department with the residency program. The handoff's flag and the 15 September draft point to General Surgery, which the grant would then need to change.

Duplicated texts and shared unsourced claims:

- Row 5, duplicate-text, confirmed (celebrate-life-flyer-noutom and celebrate-life-scoliosis-night-julcos): Who coaches: identical line in both flyers. Carry the line once, flagged as unconfirmed until the players or club commitment is named (see candidate 8).
- Row 6, duplicate-text, confirmed (celebrate-life-flyer-noutom and celebrate-life-scoliosis-night-julcos): Venue, contacts, registration form and event page repeated across both flyers. Carry the shared details once. Note that the earlier oncology flyer still points to the live form, so it should be withdrawn or the form should record which flyer a sign-up came from.
- Row 7, duplicate-text, confirmed (celebrate-life-flyer-noutom and celebrate-life-scoliosis-night-julcos): Sideline and costume lines repeated. Carry the scoliosis flyer's wording, which includes the earlier lines, as one text.
- Row 8, unsourced-shared-claim, confirmed (celebrate-life-flyer-noutom and celebrate-life-scoliosis-night-julcos; review: held): Professional players from Southeast Michigan coach the children. Record the club's written commitment (which players, which team) before the textbook or a funding request cites the coaches.
- Row 9, unsourced-shared-claim, confirmed (celebrate-life-flyer-noutom and celebrate-life-scoliosis-night-julcos): Celebrate Life runs alongside a Fieldhouse Halloween event on October 24. Confirm the companion event and that night's match schedule with the venue's own listing before families are told.
- Row 24, unsourced-shared-claim, confirmed (osc-outreach-funding-kak2gk and spine-program-student-onboarding): OSC's openly released CT dataset and its archives. Put the dataset's resolved Zenodo DOI and version, and the record count read from the archive, on the funding page before applications cite it.

### Numbers

- Collision 0, confirmed-contradiction (follow-up, group re-rule: held, hint corrected), number of C-ArmLSpine cases scored in the five-fold lateral whole-frame evaluation (98.2%): or-readiness-ledger gives 148 cases, counting-from-the-sacrum gives 141 cases, 617 vertebrae. Carry the 22 September record as 98.2% exact on whole lateral frames, five-fold CV, lower bound 95.7%, with its case count flagged as 148 in the OR Readiness Ledger against 141 in Counting From The Sacrum (which alone gives 617 vertebrae); settle it from the CV fold record of the cases each fold scored. Say that the October pages give 97.4% for whole-frame naming (row 5).
- Collision 3, confirmed-contradiction (read again, was cannot-tell; follow-up: held), frames per C-ArmLSpine CBCT sweep (case134): schwingnet-data-registry gives 301 XA frames each, research-directions-from-the-data-holding-kcyckb gives 302-frame spin. Carry 301 frames per sweep and 602 in all, from the registry, which counts XA frames and agrees with the atlas's 301-item case134 bank and with the 602 total the research-directions page itself gives; a census of the case134 sweep by DICOM Modality and Rows would settle the 302.
- Collision 5, updated-later (follow-up, group re-rule: overturned, was confirmed-contradiction), exact-level naming on a whole lateral C-ArmLSpine frame: or-readiness-ledger gives 98.2%, research-directions-from-the-data-holding-kcyckb gives 97.4%. Carry 97.4% of bodies named on a whole lateral C-ArmLSpine frame (74.8% with one body) from Research Directions and the 8 October handoff, which the 21 October Any-View plan repeats; say the 22 September ledger gave 98.2%, that State of the Literature (8 October) still gives 98.2% (606 of 617), and that Research Directions and the handoff give 98.2% to the coupled count. Confirm against paper 1's results catalog.
- Collision 6, updated-later (follow-up: overturned, was confirmed-contradiction), exact-level naming with one vertebra in the lateral C-arm field: counting-from-the-sacrum gives 72.85%, handoff-schwingnet-ctspinopelvic1k-and-the-imaging-programme-rbcdmf gives 74.8%. The quotes, by collision:

- 13. The reader's: A, ninety-three-pixels: "takes physics to 92.3% and the calibrated model to 93.9%"; B, counting-levels-under-fluoroscopy: "a per-frame smooth field (the device's window, scatter pedestal, heel, vignette) brings it to 93.9% against a quantum-noise ceiling of 99.5%".
- 32. The reader's: A, schwingnet-data-registry: "16,531 digitised films without landmarks"; B, handoff-schwingnet-ctspinopelvic1k-and-the-imaging-programme-rbcdmf: "with no written reason for the other 26,996 held films".
- 36. The reader's: A, spine-program-decisions-qqhkqz: "Class-agnostic detection plus keypoints on any spine radiograph. mAP50 0.991, PCK@0.1 0.812."; B, vertebra-detector-field-report-1jljc6: "\| NHANES II film, cervical \| \| 294 \| \| 0.970 \| \| 0.815 \| \| 0.924 \|".

## Cluster by cluster

Each swept cluster has a page of its own under contradictions/, linked from the table above: every candidate with its kind, both artifacts, whether a script found the reader's quotes, the verdict, the review's outcome and the skeptic's explanation, then what each artifact says with the words the verdict rests on, the clustering's superseded relations as the reader checked them, and what the cluster asserts without a source or holds as dated.
